# Add 41 reviewed retro projects across home computers, consoles, PCs, operating systems, applications and development tools.

Add 41 reviewed retro projects across home computers, consoles, PCs, operating systems, applications and development tools.

Date: 2026-10-18 · Review: ready

Snapshot check: upstream heads were not checked; pinned evidence is a historical snapshot.

### Identity (reviewed)

Reusable command-line binary-analysis tool, not a disassembly of one game.
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/DASM80/blob/e48c052fb2150974b981c4d3a21488143bb7bb12/README.md
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/DASM80/blob/e48c052fb2150974b981c4d3a21488143bb7bb12/DASM80/DASM80.c

### Classification (reviewed)

Actual implementation has screening segments for code/byte/word/character/jump-table data, symbols, relocation offsets and LS-DOS SVC handling. User classification is needed; documentation claim of all-Z80 support not exhaustively validated. Root GPL-3.0 text coexists with README freeware/not-public-domain/all-copyrights-retained wording; preserve discrepancy.
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/DASM80/blob/e48c052fb2150974b981c4d3a21488143bb7bb12/README.md
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/DASM80/blob/e48c052fb2150974b981c4d3a21488143bb7bb12/DASM80/DASM80.c

### Source CPU (reviewed)

Documented source/input platforms: TRS-80. Processor scope: Z80.
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/DASM80/blob/e48c052fb2150974b981c4d3a21488143bb7bb12/README.md
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/DASM80/blob/e48c052fb2150974b981c4d3a21488143bb7bb12/DASM80/DASM80.c

### Target CPU (reviewed)

No fixed target CPU is established; portability of the implementation language is not proof of supported architectures.
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/DASM80/blob/e48c052fb2150974b981c4d3a21488143bb7bb12/README.md
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/DASM80/blob/e48c052fb2150974b981c4d3a21488143bb7bb12/DASM80/DASM80.c

### Build (reviewed)

Old Visual C++ 8 Win32 project; conio.h, dos.h and sys\stat.h are present. No portable build inferred; not built or run. No candidate software was compiled or executed in this pass; build/run/play/byte-identity flags remain unknown.
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/DASM80/blob/e48c052fb2150974b981c4d3a21488143bb7bb12/README.md
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/DASM80/blob/e48c052fb2150974b981c4d3a21488143bb7bb12/DASM80/DASM80.c

### Runtime profiles (no-evidence-found)

Windows CLI documented. Host CPU minimum unknown.

### Identity (reviewed)

Modern reconstruction/enhancement of Peter Cervasio LS-DOS sources, with US/French/German keyboard selection and Matthew Reed date patches through 2079.
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/TRS80_LSDOS631L/blob/f97394d482116a234930b57ab6c657d59793529b/README.md
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/TRS80_LSDOS631L/blob/f97394d482116a234930b57ab6c657d59793529b/LSDOS631L/lsdos631/sysres.asm

### Classification (reviewed)

SYSRES contains substantial labelled OS code and includes file positioning, loader, tasker and initialization modules. README explicitly says BREF/TED sources were not reconstructed by predecessor. Some 6.3.1 changes remain incompletely commented. Root GPL-3.0 does not resolve embedded rights: Roy Soltoff allows use/redistribution with notice, reserves other rights; README quotes Frank Durda IV no-mirroring restriction on incorporated keyboard source.
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/TRS80_LSDOS631L/blob/f97394d482116a234930b57ab6c657d59793529b/README.md
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/TRS80_LSDOS631L/blob/f97394d482116a234930b57ab6c657d59793529b/LSDOS631L/lsdos631/sysres.asm

### Source CPU (reviewed)

Documented source/input platforms: TRS-80 Model 4, TRS-80 Model 4P. Processor scope: Z80.
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/TRS80_LSDOS631L/blob/f97394d482116a234930b57ab6c657d59793529b/README.md
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/TRS80_LSDOS631L/blob/f97394d482116a234930b57ab6c657d59793529b/LSDOS631L/lsdos631/sysres.asm

### Target CPU (reviewed)

Documented output/support targets: TRS-80 Model 4, TRS-80 Model 4P. Processor scope: Z80.
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/TRS80_LSDOS631L/blob/f97394d482116a234930b57ab6c657d59793529b/README.md
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/TRS80_LSDOS631L/blob/f97394d482116a234930b57ab6c657d59793529b/LSDOS631L/lsdos631/sysres.asm

### Build (reviewed)

BUILD.BAT invokes included SimZ80 with boot/source/output disk images; MRAS/XREF and JCL build paths documented. Source is also plain text. Binary-identical predecessor claims must not be generalized to every Level L flavor; no build attempted. No candidate software was compiled or executed in this pass; build/run/play/byte-identity flags remain unknown.
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/TRS80_LSDOS631L/blob/f97394d482116a234930b57ab6c657d59793529b/README.md
- https://github.com/GmEsoft/TRS80_LSDOS631L/blob/f97394d482116a234930b57ab6c657d59793529b/LSDOS631L/lsdos631/sysres.asm

### Identity (reviewed)

Original author David Shadoff reconstructed his 1983/84 game from archived notes and listings, restoring comments into cross-assembly source.
- https://github.com/dshadoff/Star-Spores_CoCo/blob/51057c0b3ec6a50bfb169a468eca2ac06bf2b4af/README.md
- https://github.com/dshadoff/Star-Spores_CoCo/blob/51057c0b3ec6a50bfb169a468eca2ac06bf2b4af/Build_notes.md

### Classification (reviewed)

Substantial MAIN logic and separate drawing/sound/data modules present. Author reports functional equivalence to original; explicitly differs in unused RAM startup settings, so not byte-exact original. MIT, Copyright 1983,1984,2020 David Shadoff.
- https://github.com/dshadoff/Star-Spores_CoCo/blob/51057c0b3ec6a50bfb169a468eca2ac06bf2b4af/README.md
- https://github.com/dshadoff/Star-Spores_CoCo/blob/51057c0b3ec6a50bfb169a468eca2ac06bf2b4af/Build_notes.md

Documented source/input platforms: TRS-80 Color Computer. Processor scope: 6809.
- https://github.com/dshadoff/Star-Spores_CoCo/blob/51057c0b3ec6a50bfb169a468eca2ac06bf2b4af/README.md
- https://github.com/dshadoff/Star-Spores_CoCo/blob/51057c0b3ec6a50bfb169a468eca2ac06bf2b4af/Build_notes.md

### Target CPU (reviewed)

Documented output/support targets: TRS-80 Color Computer. Processor scope: 6809.
- https://github.com/dshadoff/Star-Spores_CoCo/blob/51057c0b3ec6a50bfb169a468eca2ac06bf2b4af/README.md
- https://github.com/dshadoff/Star-Spores_CoCo/blob/51057c0b3ec6a50bfb169a468eca2ac06bf2b4af/Build_notes.md

### Build (reviewed)

LWTOOLS lwasm assembles modules. Final output needs RAM preformat, LOADM of modules and SAVEM in CoCo/emulator. Original EDTASM route fails to accommodate largest module unless patched; author could not get old patched EDTASM working in XRoar. Not built. No candidate software was compiled or executed in this pass; build/run/play/byte-identity flags remain unknown.
- https://github.com/dshadoff/Star-Spores_CoCo/blob/51057c0b3ec6a50bfb169a468eca2ac06bf2b4af/README.md
- https://github.com/dshadoff/Star-Spores_CoCo/blob/51057c0b3ec6a50bfb169a468eca2ac06bf2b4af/Build_notes.md

### Runtime profiles (no-evidence-found)

Original development on 32K CoCo with Extended Color BASIC and disk hardware. Main includes a Dragon 32 keyboard-detection patch with author warning it may not work on all Dragons; do not claim broad verified Dragon support.
- https://github.com/dshadoff/Star-Spores_CoCo/blob/51057c0b3ec6a50bfb169a468eca2ac06bf2b4af/README.md
- https://github.com/dshadoff/Star-Spores_CoCo/blob/51057c0b3ec6a50bfb169a468eca2ac06bf2b4af/Build_notes.md

Actual Python opcode table and recursive-descent design inspected. Untraced and unsupported/undocumented opcodes emitted as .byte; round-trip equality does not prove executable coverage. Coverage document says 5536/8192 bytes annotated (67.6%), 2656 bare; source explicitly marks hypotheses with ??/(inferred), NEXT_STEPS retains unresolved verification items. README completion marks must not become semantic-completeness claim. No LICENSE file in inspected tree; README educational/fair-use assertions are not a general license. Game instruction rights remain publisher-owned; docs reference GPLv2+ Altirra provenance.

Readable 3-voice synthesis state, pitch/frequency/mixer tables, timer interrupt assembly and C bindings. Some fields intentionally retain cryptic names. This reconstructs sound driver, not full G.I.S.T editor. Binary-embedded driver option is one documented path; direct source linkage also exists. README explicitly states all contents uploaded with G.I.S.T author permission; original 1986 Synthetic Software copyright retained. No general open-source license established.

701-line ELF relocation/X-header converter and substantial serial/TCP debugger bridge inspected, plus IOCS/DOS tests. Not merely tool links. README compares ABI/tradeoffs with other toolchains. Tests are supplied, no observed passing result from this audit. No top-level license found for orchestration/custom converter/bridge; fetched GNU/newlib/vasm components have their own licenses. Do not assign one blanket GPL license.

BBC BASIC/inline ARM patches add half-speed high-density floppy support to original Archimedes 1772-based ADFS module. Software patch work distinct from linked drive PCB project.

Readable commented ARM branch insertion, format-detection heuristic, controller bitmap and timeout modifications. Existing ADFS remains opaque binary substrate. Original ADFS and linked hardware retain separate rights.

Substantive multi-pass instruction/data discrimination and user-specified entry/data hints, 68851/68881/68882 support; Mac A-line tables and resource-fork output pipeline. Source headers explicitly grant use/copy/modify/distribute for any purpose without fee with copyright/permission notices retained; not just all-rights-reserved. No SPDX label asserted.

Zelda3 — A Link to the Past C reconstruction and native port

### Classification (reviewed)

README says 70–80k lines of C and playable start-to-end. src/player.c contains real gameplay; src/zelda_rtl.c calls ZeldaRunFrameInternal when no verification emulator callback is present. LakeSnes-derived PPU/DSP hardware rendering remains; optional original 65C816 execution compares RAM each frame for verification. This qualifies as native source reconstruction, not an emulator-only repackaging. README declares MIT for project code; original game resources must be supplied separately and retain their own rights.

Super Smash Bros. Melee — matching GameCube decompilation

### Classification (reviewed)

README describes the game as fully decompiled and now shiftable, with remaining effort naming/cleanup. configure.py contains 1,117 Object(Matching, ...) entries and no Object(NonMatching/Equivalent, ...) uses at this pin. Actual lbcommand.c implements readable game command logic. Scope is main.dol and included SDK/library reconstruction, not all disc assets or a native desktop game port. Original data extracts remain necessary; no opaque executable fallback was found in sampled matching-object configuration, but no whole-tree binary audit was performed. No project-wide license established in the reviewed root entries. Commercial assets and reconstructed vendor libraries retain separate rights.

PSn00bSDK — open PlayStation SDK and native toolchain

- https://github.com/Lameguy64/PSn00bSDK/blob/5d9aa2d3dfc7d6e51c2eb942ab4cdbae5571a40a/LICENSE.md

### Classification (reviewed)

Implements core Sony SDK-like functionality with hardware-level GPU/GTE/CD/MDEC/serial APIs. README says this is not a complete or drop-in official SDK replacement. System 573 support is preliminary. Major provenance caveat: README explicitly says parts of libpsxgte assembly were lifted as-is from Sony libraries and calls the project a legal gray area. MPL 2.0 core; examples/template additional reuse exception; mkpsxiso/dumpsxiso GPLv2+ and bundled GCC GPLv3. Preserve upstream lifted-Sony-code warning alongside license labels.

Source reimplementation of MS-DOS DEBUG, extending real-mode and DPMI debugging and built-in assembly/disassembly.

### Classification (reviewed)

Reimplements rather than restores Microsoft DEBUG source. Documents missing .HEX loading, CPU-aware instructions through P6, FPU and partial DEBUGX MMX; 32-bit registers need 386+. ### Identity (reviewed)

General-purpose PalmOS emulator, with DragonBall PalmOS 1–4 and ARM Tungsten E2/PalmOS 5 emulation, a browser frontend and native developer/debugger applications. Not a game-specific wrapper.
- https://github.com/cloudpilot-emu/cloudpilot-emu/blob/064d5d578bcd1f9072de384d05aa217f83db2f7f/README.md
- https://github.com/cloudpilot-emu/cloudpilot-emu/blob/064d5d578bcd1f9072de384d05aa217f83db2f7f/src/Makefile

### Classification (reviewed)

README documents supported devices, known emulation limitations and GDB/CLI support. Native OS4 developer frontend omits skins/audio. OS5 keyboard, clipboard and networking integration are not yet available. EmCPU68K.cpp contains the actual POSE/UAE guest instruction infrastructure, distinct from host output CPU. Browser/modern native portability is not evidence of any original-handheld runnable output. README declares GPL-3.0-or-later for project; preserved EmCPU68K.cpp carries Palm POSE GPL-2.0-or-later header; bundled components have separate licenses.
- https://github.com/cloudpilot-emu/cloudpilot-emu/blob/064d5d578bcd1f9072de384d05aa217f83db2f7f/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

PalmOS API/system reimplementation for modern hosts, running legacy PalmOS applications without an original PalmOS ROM. Native C subsystem implementations coexist with embedded 68K and ARM application execution.
- https://github.com/migueletto/PumpkinOS/blob/afb8e080a849595ffa14e207137cdd1a2583e511/README.md
- https://github.com/migueletto/PumpkinOS/blob/afb8e080a849595ffa14e207137cdd1a2583e511/src/Makefile

### Classification (reviewed)

README labels system experimental. It explicitly says no PalmOS ROM is required and m68K applications can run. Event.c is actual native event subsystem implementation; libpumpkin/Makefile lists extensive native API modules and ARM/68K emulator sources. armemu.c initializes a PXA255-compatible ARM guest and calls cpuCycle, so ARM is guest scope as well as a separately documented modern host family. Do not classify this as original Palm ROM source restoration or pure hardware emulator. README declares GPL v3 and points to component license directory; third-party Palm SDK/application and emulator licenses must be retained. Not a claim that original PalmOS source or ROM is redistributed under GPL.
- https://github.com/migueletto/PumpkinOS/blob/afb8e080a849595ffa14e207137cdd1a2583e511/README.md
- https://github.com/migueletto/PumpkinOS/blob/afb8e080a849595ffa14e207137cdd1a2583e511/src/Makefile

### Source CPU (reviewed)

Documented source/input platforms: Palm OS. Processor scope: m68k, ARM.

- https://github.com/migueletto/PumpkinOS/blob/afb8e080a849595ffa14e207137cdd1a2583e511/src/Makefile

### Target CPU (reviewed)

Documented output/support targets: Linux, Windows, Haiku, Web. Processor scope: x86, x86-64, ARM, AArch64, WebAssembly.
- https://github.com/migueletto/PumpkinOS/blob/afb8e080a849595ffa14e207137cdd1a2583e511/README.md
- https://github.com/migueletto/PumpkinOS/blob/afb8e080a849595ffa14e207137cdd1a2583e511/src/Makefile

### Build (reviewed)

README documents GCC/binutils/make/git and SDL2, src make producing pumpkin executable, libraries and PRCs; Linux/WSL2 and MSYS2/MINGW64 instructions. Current src/Makefile also explicitly selects Haiku and Emscripten modules. common.mak names x86/ARM/AArch64 machine cases and Emscripten output; these are build-config support, not tested outputs. libpumpkin/Makefile links native PalmOS manager implementations together with 68K and ARM compatibility code. No build or execution performed. No candidate software was compiled or executed in this pass; build/run/play/byte-identity flags remain unknown.

Its instructions focus on 64-bit Windows/Linux; additional CPU branches in common.mak do not establish minimum hardware. Native 68K/ARM cores execute guest application instructions, not native target outputs.

### Relationships (reviewed)

Uses third-party Musashi, uARM and optional Unicorn components, evidenced by licenses and libpumpkin build/source. Distinct from hardware/ROM-based CloudpilotEmu. No current tracker URL match.
- https://github.com/migueletto/PumpkinOS/blob/afb8e080a849595ffa14e207137cdd1a2583e511/README.md
- https://github.com/migueletto/PumpkinOS/blob/afb8e080a849595ffa14e207137cdd1a2583e511/src/Makefile

## Research event notes

Broad discovery followed platform catalogues, developer portfolios, source-preservation and tooling links, with programmatic URL screening before detailed inspection. This is a diverse bounded pass, not an exhaustive inventory of every platform. Quality and completeness caveats remain explicit; preserved source, native reconstruction, homebrew and modern tooling are distinguished. No candidate code was executed. All earlier catalogue records are preserved.
